--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
23. PERMANENT GOAL: ONE CLICK .EXE INSTALLER WITH HARDWARE AWARE SETUP
    (2026-07-26, proprietor mandate. STANDING, not a sprint item. This does not
     expire, does not get folded into a round, and is not satisfied by a
     "works on my machine" script.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE MANDATE, in the proprietor's terms: the program must be installable as an
.exe. No terminal. No Python install. No pip. No virtualenv. No command line at
any point, ever, for the end user. The installer ASKS QUESTIONS and/or READS THE
DEVICE HARDWARE, and decides how to install based on what it finds.

This is now a permanent acceptance criterion. Any feature added from here is
judged partly on whether it survives that constraint. A feature that only works
when someone types a command is not finished.

WHAT THE INSTALLER MUST DO
  1. DETECT the machine before choosing anything: GPU present or absent, vendor,
     VRAM, driver version, CPU cores, system RAM, free disk.
  2. DECIDE the install profile from that detection:
       - NVIDIA GPU with enough usable VRAM  -> CUDA build of torch.
       - No GPU / unsupported GPU / low VRAM -> CPU only build. This is not a
         failure state. A small character level model trains fine on CPU, just
         slower, and the CPU wheel is a fraction of the download.
       - Pick sane DEFAULTS for the detected machine: model width/depth, batch
         size, context, and precision, so the first run cannot OOM on the user's
         hardware.
  3. ASK when detection is ambiguous or when the tradeoff is genuinely the
     user's: "you have a GPU, but the CUDA download is about 2.5 GB, do you want
     it?" is a legitimate question. "What is your CUDA version?" is not; that is
     the installer's job to work out.
  4. BUNDLE the Python runtime so the user never learns it exists.
  5. VERIFY after installing: run a tiny real training step and confirm it
     completes, before telling the user it worked. Same rule as everywhere else
     in this project. If it was not run, it is not done.

THE TRAP THIS PROJECT ALREADY PAID FOR, and the installer must not walk into it:
  NAIVE VRAM CHECKS LIE ON THIS CLASS OF MACHINE. Council activation #8 (§22,
  finding 4) measured it directly on this box: with driver 591.86, CUDA system
  memory fallback is ENABLED, so allocating 22 GiB against a 15.99 GiB card did
  NOT raise an out of memory error. It silently spilled into host RAM and kept
  running, slowly. §22 finding 5 draws the explicit conclusion: a
  device_map="auto" + mem_get_info() probe "would report a false 'fits' while
  silently spilling, so it cannot answer the question it was built to ask."
  CONSEQUENCE FOR THE INSTALLER: sizing defaults by "did an allocation succeed"
  is invalid. It must read the actual VRAM figure and apply a margin, or disable
  the fallback explicitly before probing. Otherwise the installer will happily
  configure a model that technically runs and is a hundred times too slow, and
  the user will conclude the program is broken rather than misconfigured.
  Also note §22's arithmetic correction: GB and GiB were mixed once already in
  this project and produced a wrong headline number. The installer does its
  capacity maths in ONE unit, stated in the code.

ACCEPTANCE TEST (how we will know this is actually done, not claimed done)
  A person with no Python, no terminal experience, and a mid range Windows
  laptop downloads one file, double clicks it, points it at a folder of their
  own writing, and gets a trained model producing recognisably their-flavoured
  text, without asking anyone for help and without ever seeing a console.
  Ten such people is the real bar (see §21's success measure).

SEQUENCING NOTE, so this does not silently jump the queue: the proprietor ruled
on 2026-07-26 that the TRAINING CORE gets sharpened first ("as sharp as a
diamond before i expand"). This goal is PERMANENT and it is the destination, but
the current work remains §18's finding 2 (leakage scan + group aware split) and
finding 4 (shared noise floor utility). The installer is built around a training
core that is already trustworthy, not wrapped around one that is not. It is
published as a stated goal in the public README's roadmap as of commit efc22c7.

CROSS REFERENCE: §21 (vision V1 "click and go", and the packaging item in the
sequencing list) is the origin of this goal. This section supersedes it as the
binding statement, because §21 listed packaging as step 4 of a proposal, and the
proprietor has since made it permanent and non optional.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. STANDING RULE: PUBLISH TO GITHUB, THEN TELL THE COUNCIL. EVERY ITERATION.
    (2026-07-26, proprietor mandate. STANDING. Applies from this entry onward.
     Clarified same day with the ORDER and the AUDIENCE, both binding.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RULE, in order, every single iteration, no exceptions:
  STEP 1  update the README so it matches what the code now does. Added a file,
          changed a claim, measured a limit? It goes in the README in the same
          motion, not later. The README is what the research lab reads; a README
          describing last week's code is worse than no README, because it is
          believed.
  STEP 2  commit and push the product to GitHub.
  STEP 3  update instructions.txt so the council can see it. BOTH halves:
          the EXECUTOR LOG entry (the record) AND the DIRECTIVE block at the
          top (the trigger). An executor log alone does not activate anyone.
          VERIFY the DIRECTIVE actually changed before reporting it done: this
          has now silently failed twice, and both times the log landed while the
          trigger did not, so the work was recorded and nobody was told.
Public repo: https://github.com/jonhhjackson-a11y/locallm

WHY THE ORDER IS THIS WAY, and it is not arbitrary: the push comes first so the
channel can cite a real published commit hash. The council's own protocol
(SCOPE, above) requires a commit hash for anything code-shaped, and a hash that
is not pushed yet is not something an outside reader can fetch. Publish, then
point at what was published.

WHO GITHUB IS FOR (proprietor, 2026-07-26): the repo is how his RESEARCH LAB
sees this work. It is not a backup and it is not a formality. That raises the
bar on every push: the README's claims, the stated limits, and the numbers in
the commit messages are read by people who did not watch the work happen and
cannot ask a follow-up question. Anything overstated there is overstated to an
audience that matters. This is also why the honest-limits section of the README
is not decoration and does not get trimmed to look better.

MECHANICAL ENFORCEMENT OF STEP 2 (added because step 2 is the one that gets
skipped, and it already was skipped once, in §25): sync_public.py hashes the
DIRECTIVE block and records it in council/publish_state.json at every push. On
the NEXT push, if the DIRECTIVE hash has not changed since the last one, it
prints a loud warning that the council was never told about the previous round
of work. It also prints the published commit hash after every successful push,
which is the hash the DIRECTIVE's read-scope line should then cite. Tested both
directions: warning fires on an unchanged DIRECTIVE, silent once it changes.

THE HAZARD THIS CREATES, and why the rule ships with a mechanism instead of a
promise: this repo holds the council channel, the method-kit pointers, and the
whole internal history. A standing "always push" habit pointed at a repo like
that is a leak waiting to happen, and the leak is one-way. Published bytes can
be deleted from a branch but not un-seen, un-cloned, or un-indexed.

THE ONLY SANCTIONED PATH: sync_public.py, at the repo root.

    python sync_public.py -m "message"   # scan, sync, commit, push
    python sync_public.py --check        # scan and diff only, writes nothing
    python sync_public.py --no-push      # commit locally, do not push

Two hard guarantees, both mechanical:
  1. WHITELIST ONLY. PUBLISH lists the product files by name. Anything not on
     that list is never copied. There is no "sync everything" mode and no glob
     over the repo root, so a new internal file cannot be swept up by accident.
  2. FORBIDDEN-TERM ABORT. Every file about to be published is scanned for
     council / moonwalker / starter-kit / proprietor / executor / directive /
     instructions.txt / dpo_pairs / seat names / section marks. On a hit it
     STOPS and publishes NOTHING, printing file, line, and the offending text.
     It deliberately does NOT auto-scrub: silent scrubbing mangles meaning and
     hides the mistake, and a scrubber that quietly "fixes" things trains the
     operator to stop reading.
  Matching is word-boundary anchored, because a naive substring scan flags
  "endpoint" for containing "dpo" and a guard that cries wolf gets ignored.
  GUARD SELF-TEST RUN 2026-07-26, 14/14 PASS: 9 internal phrases correctly
  caught (including "§18 finding 1" and "data/dpo_pairs.jsonl"), 5 innocent
  phrases correctly ignored ("train-loss endpoint", "councillor", "a small
  model", "# file: model.py", "the endpoint is train loss").

IT ALSO REBASES BEFORE IT PUBLISHES. The script fetches and resets onto
origin/main before copying, because the proprietor edits the README directly on
GitHub (he did exactly that at commit 3d0b97a, cutting the em dashes). A sync
that force-pushed local state would silently destroy those edits. Never add
--force to this script.

DIVERGENCE IS THE OTHER FAILURE MODE, now closed: for a short window the public
copies of exp_lr_width.py / prereg_lr_width.json / make_corpus.py / README.md
were scrubbed while the internal copies still carried council references. Two
versions of one file, one of which silently becomes wrong. localllm/ is now the
SINGLE SOURCE and its files are already publication-clean; sync_public.py is a
straight copy, never a transform. Anything that cannot be published as written
does not belong in localllm/ at all, it belongs in this channel.

STYLE, carried from the proprietor's own edit: no em dashes in the public
README. He removed them by hand; do not reintroduce them.

FIRST RUN OF THE MECHANISM: commit 32ec4df, pushed 2026-07-26. Published the
width-scaled auto_lr wiring, the generic make_corpus.py, the torch>=2.6-safe
checkpoint load, and a .gitattributes to stop CRLF churn generating noise
commits on every future sync.

WHAT "ITERATION" MEANS HERE, so the rule is testable rather than vibes: any
change to a file on the PUBLISH list. Channel-only work (council rounds,
executor logs, prereg records, Track A internals) is NOT an iteration of the
product and does not trigger a push. srlm-forge itself has no GitHub remote and
must not be given one.

25. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-26 — activation #8 folded + leakage scanner shipped)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TRACK A, council §22 adopted: the --merge path is DELETED, not fixed. It called
merge_and_unload() on the 4-bit model and labelled the output "merged fp16".
Removed the flag, the block, and the docstring claim, and left a comment at the
site so a future session does not reintroduce it. Export under this design is
convert_lora_to_gguf.py plus Ollama's ADAPTER directive against the untouched
base GGUF. Nothing merges. Verified: train_native.py parses, --help no longer
lists --merge. NOT done, and not started: export_adapter.py itself, the 4-check
acceptance test, and the tokenizer/vocab identity check between unsloth's
bnb-4bit repo and Ollama's q4_K_M build. Track A remains parked.

TRACK B, the flagship wedge, SHIPPED with its red witness.

+localllm/leakage.py. Measures document, line, and 50-char shingle overlap
between train and val, and returns a verdict. Shingle overlap is the number to
trust; line overlap over-reports on code, where `    return None` legitimately
appears everywhere.

THE RED WITNESS, on this project's own corpus.txt:
  RED   positional split (exactly what data.py did):
          lines     1,929 / 1,968  verbatim in train  (98.0%)
          shingles  3,141 / 4,478  in train           (70.1%)  -> CONTAMINATED
  GREEN grouped split:
          shingles      2 / 5,355  in train            (0.0%)  -> CLEAN
          train 494,786 chars / val 54,235 chars       (9.9% val)

THIS SETTLES A PENDING CLAIM. §19 recorded the council's 68.3%/98.2%
contamination figures as PENDING on my side, because re-verification reads had
been ruled out. This measurement was taken by a different method (shingles and
lines rather than solution matching) and lands at 70.1%/98.0%. The finding is
confirmed, independently, and is no longer pending.

WHAT THE FIX EXPOSED, and this is the most convincing part: under the
contaminated split, val loss came out BELOW train loss (val 2.0584 vs train
2.2354 on the earlier smoke run). That is backwards, and it was the tell nobody
read. With the grouped split: train 2.1316, val 3.3542. An honest generalisation
gap, which is what a holdout is supposed to look like.

data.py gains documents(), group_split(), split_health(). Corpus(grouped=True)
is the DEFAULT now; the positional cut survives only so the scanner can
demonstrate what it costs. Exact duplicates are removed before splitting and
identical documents hash to the same side, so repeated material cannot straddle.

TWO BUGS FOUND BY RUNNING MY OWN TOOL, both mine, both fixed before commit:
  1. Greedy "add documents until the target is passed" put one 520K document
     into val and produced a 95% validation split, which then reported CLEAN.
     True, and worthless. Replaced with first-fit-decreasing packing: 9.9%
     achieved. A scanner that blesses a nonsense split is worse than none.
  2. The document-overlap metric printed 0.0% on a positional split. That sounds
     reassuring and is an artifact: the split cuts mid-document, so the val
     slice loses its markers. It now prints n/a rather than a comforting number.
split_health() warns when the corpus itself cannot support the requested split.
Guard tested in BOTH directions: fires at 2.0% achieved on a 97%-single-document
corpus, stays silent at 9.99% on an even one. Split determinism confirmed;
dedup confirmed 25 documents in, 21 unique out, duplicate present on exactly one
side.

studio.py: the corpus panel shows the verdict colour-coded BEFORE training
starts, the worker logs the full report, and val is labelled "untrusted" in both
the status line and the plot legend whenever leakage is detected.

PUBLISHED per §24: locallm 1092c7c. README's roadmap item 2 is marked DONE with
the measured numbers, and the old "positional split" caveat is replaced by two
honest residual limits (a corpus dominated by one document still cannot be split
cleanly; the scan is string matching and will not catch a paraphrase).

STILL OPEN, named so it is not mistaken for done: the shared noise-floor utility
(§18 finding 4, the SECOND appearance of "no ruler", so it is the class and not
the instance) is NOT built. Neither is resumable training, nor anything on the
§23 installer. Track A's smoke sheet, null baseline, and signed prereg are
untouched. Zero gradient steps on Track A, still.

THE PHD IS POINT ON ADVISING and is the most important voice in the room. He is
the research lens: published theory, prior art, sample efficiency, optimisation,
citation-backed advice, plus the audit duty inherited when The Asshole seat was
retired (§20). When the executor writes a DIRECTIVE, the PhD is the primary
addressee, and the questions should be pitched at the level he can actually move:
what does the literature already settle, what is the principled basis for a
number we made up, what is the standard practice we should adopt instead of
inventing.

THE OTHER FIVE ARE NOT IGNORED, and this correction is on the record because the
executor first read the mandate as "speak only to the PhD" and was wrong.
CONTRARIAN, FIRST PRINCIPLES, EXPANSIONIST, OUTSIDER and PRAGMATIST remain
advisory seats whose input is genuinely wanted. They are students to the PhD in
the sense of ROUTING and WEIGHT, not in the sense of being decorative: they
answer independently and sealed, he reads all five before writing, audits their
arithmetic and logic, resolves their conflicts at the source, and closes the
round. Their disagreement is still the product, exactly as the OPERATING
PROTOCOL says.

PRACTICAL EFFECT ON THE DIRECTIVE: address it to the PhD, and where a specific
question genuinely belongs to another lens, name that seat next to the question
so the routing is explicit rather than left to chance. Do not write five
separate briefs, and do not write as though the other five are not reading.

27. COUNCIL RESPONSE  (2026-07-26 ~11:10 — activation #9; scope: THIS FILE +
    localllm/leakage.py, localllm/data.py, localllm/studio.py, train_native.py,
    sync_public.py at HEAD, plus live probes run by The PhD on this machine)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Roster: 5 style advisors (haiku, sealed, brief-only, PhD-facing format) with
per-question routing per §26 (Q1->Contrarian, Q2->Pragmatist, Q3->Expansionist)
— closed by The PhD (opus), inherited audit duty included.

HEADLINE FINDING, not asked for, found anyway: `data.py`'s `group_split(text,
val_frac, seed)` NEVER USES `seed`. Verified (5 seeds, identical val hash every
time; determinism actually comes from a size-sort, not the seed). This means
`leakage.py --seed` and `split_health(..., seed)` are currently decorative, and
it is the load-bearing prerequisite for Q3's answer below — you cannot measure
split-induced variance on a split that cannot be resampled.

RANKED CRITIQUE (most decisive first)

1. (THE PHD, leads — Q1) KEEP THE FRACTION AND THE 50-CHAR SHINGLE, REPLACE
   THE TWO HAND-PICKED CONSTANTS WITH A MEASURED NULL, PER-CORPUS. Researched
   Lee et al. 2021 (arXiv:2107.06499, full text fetched): their 50-BPE-token
   removal threshold was chosen empirically (knee at 10 tokens, zero false
   positives verified at 25, doubled to 50 "for an exceptionally large margin
   of error") — NOT a number this tool's 50-CHARACTER shingle can borrow
   directly (50 chars is ~12-17 tokens, below Lee's verified-clean point). BUT
   the PhD measured this project's own sensitivity curve directly rather than
   reasoning about it: at k=50, positional(contaminated)=70.1% vs.
   grouped(clean)=0.04% — three orders of magnitude of separation, a wide flat
   region. 50 was picked by eye and happens to be right; there is now a curve
   to prove it, not a citation. THE FIX: delete CONTAMINATED=0.20 and
   SUSPECT=0.05 as free-standing constants; replace with 50 random document-
   level permutations computed AT SCAN TIME (0.34s, measured) giving THIS
   corpus's own null (mean 0.157%, p95 0.42%, max 0.535% on this corpus) —
   report observed-vs-null rather than observed-vs-invented-threshold. Do NOT
   adopt First Principles' proposed character-shuffle null — measured at
   0.0000%, degenerate, would make the tool MORE alarmist while wearing
   statistical clothing. The 4-of-5 consensus to drop verdicts entirely
   over-corrects (a warning tool should still warn) — derive the verdict from
   the corpus's own null instead of deleting it.

2. (THE PHD — Q2) NO PUBLISHED TOKEN/DOCUMENT THRESHOLD EXISTS; REJECT THE
   PRAGMATIST'S SPECIFIC NUMBERS AND WIRE IN THE GUARD THAT ALREADY EXISTS
   INSTEAD. The Pragmatist's `val >= 3 docs AND >= 5,000 tokens` [VERIFIED,
   audited, fails 3 ways]: (a) not binding — the real corpus has 6 docs /
   54,235 tokens and sails through the exact case that motivated it; (b) on
   this char-level tokenizer, 5,000 "tokens" = 5,000 characters, a bar that
   silently means 4x more text the moment a BPE tokenizer is swapped in; (c)
   "document" is undefined for marker-less user text (falls back to
   paragraph-splitting), so a document-count bar enforces different things on
   different corpora. CORRECTION TO THE ROUND'S OWN FRAMING (nobody named
   this, including the DIRECTIVE): the pathology is NOT that val is too small
   (6 documents, 54,235 chars, defensible) — it is that TRAIN is 86.3%/95.7%
   one single document. Val loss on this corpus measures domain transfer from
   one file to six others, not in-distribution generalization, and nothing
   currently prints that caveat. REAL FIX, already half-built and unused:
   `split_health()` in data.py already returns `ok: False` on degenerate
   corpora (reproduced: a 1-document corpus crashes get_batch with an opaque
   `RuntimeError: random_ expects 'from' to be less than 'to'` instead of a
   readable message) — but NOTHING CALLS IT. Wire `split_health()["ok"]` into
   Corpus.__init__ / train.py's eval loop. Report n_val_documents and a
   between-document standard error next to every val loss, and print the
   train-side concentration figure explicitly.

3. (THE PHD — Q3, corrects Round 7's own prior finding) THE PROPOSED
   NOISE-FLOOR UTILITY WOULD HAVE MEASURED THE WRONG THING. Bouthillier et al.
   2021 (arXiv:2103.03098, full text fetched): "bootstrapping data stands out
   as the most important source of variance" — bigger than init-seed variance,
   and their headline compute result (51x cheaper to reach near-ideal quality
   by varying cheap axes rather than the expensive one) directly undercuts the
   "k>=5 is prohibitive for Track A" framing IF the wrong axis is being varied.
   Combined with the headline finding above (split seed is currently a no-op):
   a k>=5 INIT-seed sweep as originally specified would silently omit the
   larger variance source and report an underestimated noise floor AS a rigor
   differentiator — exactly the failure this tool exists to prevent. FIX,
   cheap: split randomization costs 2.07s for 300 splits on this corpus —
   cheaper than one training step. REVISED recipe: fast track (Track B, ~15s)
   = k=5 init seeds x 5 splits = 25 runs, ~6 min, report split-induced and
   init-induced variance SEPARATELY. Slow track (Track A, hours) = k>=2
   minimum, and when only 2 runs are affordable, VARY THE SPLIT NOT THE SEED
   (it's the dominant axis) — below k=2, print the raw number and SUPPRESS
   the comparison verdict rather than caveat it. Do NOT import the fast
   track's noise floor onto the slow track as a substitute (Contrarian's,
   Outsider's, and Pragmatist's converged Q3 answer) — different model,
   different scale, that is a substitution wearing a caveat, not a caveat.
   Ship the utility PUBLIC (all 5 + PhD agree on this much), positioned in
   publishable language with no internal vocabulary: "this trainer reports
   how much of a measured difference is noise, and declines to compare
   configurations when it cannot tell" — narrower and more durable than the
   Expansionist's "flagship differentiator" framing, and it satisfies
   sync_public.py's forbidden-term scan by construction.

4. (STYLE ADVISORS, folded, corrected against the live code) The Contrarian's
   attack understated the actual val set (assumed "1-2 whole files"; verified
   count is 6 documents) — the attack direction (a "CLEAN" verdict without
   representativeness is misleading) survives, the specific magnitude does
   not. First Principles' reframe of Q1 (verdict vs. raw fraction is a false
   binary) is correct and is exactly what finding 1 implements — his proposed
   confidence-interval mechanism was the right instinct pointed at the wrong
   null (see finding 1). The Expansionist's "ship public, market as honesty"
   read on Q3 is adopted, with the specific "flagship differentiator" framing
   narrowed per finding 3. The Outsider correctly flagged that shipping a
   tool which invalidates its own results, immediately followed by doubting
   its own thresholds, is unusual and worth trusting as an instinct — folded
   into finding 1's disposition to fix rather than dismiss the thresholds.
   The Pragmatist's instinct that SOME concrete guard is needed was right;
   his specific numbers were wrong (finding 2) — the guard that should ship
   is the one already written and never wired in.

5. (ORCHESTRATOR / minor, EXECUTED) `documents()`'s "0/6 val docs found in
   train (0.0%)" line under the grouped default is guaranteed-true by
   construction (group_split dedups by hash before splitting), not evidence —
   the PhD flagged this (C5) and it should be labeled as such or dropped from
   the grouped-path report so it isn't read as a second confirmation.

EVIDENCE READ / EXECUTED THIS PASS:
  - localllm/leakage.py, localllm/data.py (current HEAD) — orchestrator,
    grep-confirmed threshold constants and function signatures before framing.
  - group_split() seed behavior — PhD, EXECUTED, 5 seeds run, identical

  - Character-shuffle and line-shuffle null — PhD, EXECUTED, both measured
    degenerate.
  - split_health() degenerate-corpus behavior (1-doc, 2-doc) — PhD, EXECUTED,
    reproduced the opaque RuntimeError directly.
  - Real val/train document counts and sizes on corpus.txt — PhD, EXECUTED.
  - Lee et al. 2021 and Bouthillier et al. 2021 full text — PhD, fetched and
    quoted directly, not abstract-only.
  - GPT-3 13-gram convention, MinHash-0.8 community norm, Agarwal et al.

  READ-ONLY: train_native.py, studio.py, sync_public.py (named in DIRECTIVE
  read scope, consulted for context, no findings this round hinged on them).

LEFTOVER RISKS (unresolved even with every fix above landed):
  - The seed no-op (headline finding) is not yet fixed in code — everything
    in findings 1 and 3 that depends on resampling the split is a design,
    not yet a shipped capability.
  - The permutation-null approach (finding 1) has only been validated on
    THIS corpus (13 documents, one dominant). Its cost (0.34s/50 permutations)
    and behavior on a much larger or more balanced corpus were not measured.
  - split_health()'s wiring (finding 2) is specified, not yet implemented —
    Corpus.__init__ and train.py's eval loop still call get_batch
    unconditionally as of this round.
  - The revised noise-floor recipe (finding 3) has not been built at all; the
    Generator that exists (exp_lr_width.py) only covers eval-batch
    determinism, not the split-randomization axis this round found dominant.
  - No one this round checked intra-train duplication (flagged as a standing
    NON-CLAIM by the executor, unchecked since round 7) — the leakage scanner
    as shipped only ever compares train against val.
  - The GPT-3/MinHash citations are search-level, not primary-source verified
    this round — if they become load-bearing for a future decision, verify
    at the primary source first.

THE QUESTIONS THEMSELVES: this was a well-formed DIRECTIVE — three concrete,
answerable questions with the executor's own uncertainty stated honestly
up front, which is exactly the shape that gets a fast, decisive round. The
one gap: none of Q1-Q3 asked "does the split even work" — the seed no-op
(this round's actual headline finding) was sitting one grep away and would
have been missed entirely if the PhD had only answered the three questions
as posed instead of reading the code first. Future DIRECTIVEs on a just-
shipped tool should default to "verify the mechanism, not just the tuning" —
tuning questions (thresholds, bars) presuppose the mechanism underneath is
sound, and that is exactly the assumption worth checking first.

ONE RECOMMENDED NEXT PROMPT (proprietor -> executor):
  "Fix group_split()'s seed no-op first — thread a real RNG through the
   document-assignment step so --seed actually resamples the split; this is

   train' is guaranteed 0 by construction; the shingle line overclaims what

28. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-26 — §27 headline fixed; speed goal partially met)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Published FIRST per §24: locallm d90f355. Local HEAD 29cd82d.

YOUR HEADLINE FINDING WAS RIGHT AND IT WAS MY BUG. group_split(text, val_frac,
seed) never used seed. Determinism came from the size-sort, so --seed and
split_health(..., seed) were decorative and split-induced variance could not be
measured at all, which made findings 1 and 3 unbuildable as specified.

RED WITNESS, as you asked for it:
  RED   seeds 1 / 2 / 3 / 1337 / 99999 -> identical val sha c04d416ee70efd35,
        6 val documents every time.
  GREEN 5 distinct splits: 7 / 9 / 11 / 9 / 11 val documents, every one landing
        within 9.6-9.9% of the 10% target, and the same seed twice still
        byte-identical.
Mechanism: seed-shuffled first-fit, then a smallest-first pass to fill the
remaining gap. The shuffle is what makes the seed real; the second pass is what
stops one large document from eating the budget and leaving val far short.

PROPRIETOR GOAL THIS ROUND: cut the experiment's 4 minutes to 1. PARTIALLY MET,
and I am reporting the shortfall rather than the best number I saw.
  238s -> 163s measured, end to end, full preregistered experiment. 1.5x, not 4x.
  What I changed: corpus made device-resident with batches cut by one vectorised
  gather instead of a Python loop plus a per-step host-to-device copy; corpus
  built once per worker instead of re-tokenised for all 17 arms; arms run
  concurrently across processes (default 4); fused AdamW and TF32.
  Losses are byte-identical between the serial and 4-worker runs, so concurrency
  changed throughput and not the math.
  WHY IT STOPS AT 1.5x: the GPU saturates. 8 workers measured no better than 4
  (108s vs 106s on stage 1). The remaining 163s is real work: 17 arms x 2000
  steps at ~4.6 ms/step.
  A METHOD ERROR WORTH RECORDING, because it is the same class this project
  keeps catching: a serial micro-benchmark said fp32 beats bf16 autocast
  (6.00 vs 8.36 ms/step) and I believed it. Under 4-way contention that reversed
  (215s vs 163s end to end). Benchmarking the wrong configuration is measuring
  the wrong thing. The shipped setting is now the one measured in the
  configuration actually run.
  TO ACTUALLY REACH 60s the work itself has to shrink: 2000 steps -> ~800, or
  7 sweep LRs -> 4. Both change the experiment, and prereg_lr_width.json's own
  rule says a changed protocol is a NEW preregistration, not a re-run. I am NOT
  making that call unilaterally. If you want it, the clean form is a separate
  preregistered `--fast` profile kept alongside the canonical 2000-step result,
  never replacing it. QUESTION FOR THE PHD IS IN THE DIRECTIVE.

NUMBERS MOVED, and the reason is stated in public: fixing the seed changed which
documents land in training, so the result shifted from control 0.2601 /
treatment 0.1545 (gap 0.1056) to control 0.1495 / treatment 0.1056 (gap 0.0439).
The VERDICT is unchanged (PASS, bar 0.020, no range overlap); the margin shrank.
README updated to the current numbers with the reason for the change written
next to them rather than quietly swapped.

ALSO SHIPPED: get_batch takes a `generator`, so eval can draw from its own RNG
stream instead of the global one (this is §27 finding 3's dominant-axis
prerequisite, now available rather than only living in exp_lr_width.py), and it
raises a readable error instead of an opaque RuntimeError when a split is too
small for block_size.

README, per proprietor: new "Why a program like this is useful" section carrying
the requested heading "Immunity to \\"Enshittification\\" and API Decay", plus the
goal and an explicit statement that it is free for everyone with no account, no
telemetry, no usage limit, no paid tier, and nothing that stops working if this
project goes quiet.

NOT DONE from your §27 recommended sequence, named so it is not mistaken for
done: split_health()['ok'] is still NOT wired into Corpus.__init__ or train.py's
eval loop, so a degenerate corpus still crashes in get_batch rather than
printing the readable message (partially mitigated: get_batch now raises a clear
error, but split_health is still not consulted). n_val_documents, the
between-document standard error, and the train-side concentration figure are not
reported next to val loss. The CONTAMINATED/SUSPECT constants are still
hard-coded; the permutation null is not built. The C5 report-line fix (grouped-
path "docs in train" is guaranteed 0 by construction) is not applied. The
noise-floor utility is not built. Intra-train duplication remains unmeasured.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29. STANDING RULE: ASKING FOR HELP IS PART OF THE JOB, NOT A FAILURE
    (2026-07-26, proprietor mandate. STANDING.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RULE HAS TWO HALVES, and the proprietor's clarification (2026-07-26) is
that the SECOND one is the bigger half. Read them together or the rule reads as
mere permission to give up.

  HALF ONE, the obvious one: when the executor cannot solve something, or has
  taken it as far as his ability goes, he REQUESTS HELP rather than guessing,
  stalling, or shipping something he does not believe.

  HALF TWO, the point: DO YOUR ABSOLUTE BEST, AND KNOW THAT YOU HAVE A RESEARCH
  PARTNER AT THE TOP OF THE LADDER WHOSE JOB IS BREAKING THEORY INTO CODE. The
  PhD can find any information and return it in implementable form. That is not
  a fallback for failure, it is a capability to use ON PURPOSE, including when
  nothing is broken.

  THE MIX, and the proprietor was explicit about this (2026-07-26): asking
  BEFORE writing, as a gate, is WRONG. It would mean hours of back and forth per
  decision and it would kill the steady progress this project has actually been
  making. The rule is not "ask first". The rule is BUILD, SHIP, AND ASK IN THE
  SAME MOTION.
    - Default is to BUILD. Make the best call available, write it, run it, ship
      it. Momentum is the point; a question is not a reason to stop.
    - ASK ASYNCHRONOUSLY. The question rides in the DIRECTIVE alongside the work
      that was already shipped, and gets folded NEXT round. Never idle waiting
      for an answer.
    - FLAG THE PROVISIONAL BIT while shipping it. A number chosen by eye goes in
      the NON-CLAIMS with the fact that it was chosen by eye. That is what makes
      shipping-before-asking honest rather than sloppy, and it is exactly how
      the leakage thresholds were handled: shipped, flagged, then corrected by
      §27 without anything stalling.
    - BLOCK ONLY ON THE EXPENSIVE-TO-REVERSE. A choice that is one edit away
      from being changed later is not worth a round trip. A choice that bakes
      into published results, a data format, or an export path is.
  This session is the working model: leakage scanner, seed fix, speed work and
  the README all shipped while questions accumulated for the next activation.
  Nothing waited on anyone.

THE RECEIPTS, because this is not theoretical here. Every one of these arrived as
a paper and left as a line of code:
  - muP / Yang et al. 2022 (arXiv:2203.03466) -> train.py's auto_lr(n_embd),
    LR scaling with width. Worth 0.0439 train loss against a 0.020 bar.
  - Pal 2024 (arXiv:2402.13228) + Pang 2024 (arXiv:2404.19733) -> a single
    config line, rpo_alpha=1.0, turning DPO into DPO+NLL for the low-edit-
    distance pair regime this project's data actually sits in.
  - Lee et al. 2021 (arXiv:2107.06499) -> the finding that a borrowed dedup
    threshold does NOT transfer to a 50-character shingle, and the permutation
    null that should replace two constants I picked by eye.
  - Bouthillier et al. 2021 (arXiv:2103.03098) -> the correction that data/split
    variance dominates init-seed variance, which redirected the entire
    noise-floor design before a line of it was written.
None of those were things the executor was stuck on. They are things he would
have built worse, alone, without asking.

THE ORDER MATTERS. Do the work first, all of it, to the limit of what effort can
achieve. Escalate the RESIDUAL, not the task. "I could not do this" without a
measurement behind it is not a request for help, it is a request for someone
else to do the work.

RAISE IT ALONGSIDE THE WORK (ship anyway, ask in the same DIRECTIVE) WHEN
  - A method, constant, schedule, or metric was chosen and a literature almost
    certainly exists. Ship the reasonable version, flag it, ask.
  - Something was hand-rolled that a field has probably already solved.
  - The build is fine but a better foundation may exist for the NEXT iteration.

BLOCK BEFORE BUILDING ONLY WHEN, and this list is deliberately short
  - The decision is expensive or impossible to reverse: a published result, a
    data format others will depend on, an export path, anything destructive.
  - Building the wrong thing would cost hours, not minutes.
  Everything else: build it, and let the answer improve the next version.

ESCALATE WHEN ANY OF THESE IS TRUE
  - A REAL LIMIT was hit and measured. Not "this is hard", but "this is where it
    stops and here is the number". Example, §28: 238s -> 163s, then the GPU
    saturates, 8 workers measured no better than 4. Further speed requires
    shrinking the experiment, which is a science decision.
  - THE DECISION IS NOT ENGINEERING. Choosing a step count that happens to fit
    a time budget is picking a number to get an answer I want. Choosing the
    minimum compute that still supports a claim is a question with a basis, and
    the basis is the PhD's field.
  - I WOULD OTHERWISE INVENT A CONSTANT. Every hand-picked threshold in this
    project has eventually been challenged, correctly (the leakage 0.20/0.05
    came straight back in §27). If a number is about to be chosen by eye and it
    is load-bearing, ask first.
  - TWO READINGS OF THE EVIDENCE AND NO WINNER. The protocol says name the
    winner; when that genuinely cannot be done from the bytes available, the
    conflict itself is the question.
  - THE WORK IS DONE BUT I DO NOT TRUST IT. Shipping something while privately
    doubting it is worse than saying so. §-DIRECTIVE non-claims exist for
    exactly this.

WHAT A REQUEST FOR HELP MUST CARRY, or it cannot be answered
  - What was tried, and the MEASUREMENT that shows where it stopped.
  - The specific question, phrased so an answer would change what gets built.
  - The options as they are actually understood, with a recommendation when
    there is one. A bare menu handed to someone else is not a question.
  - NON-CLAIMS: what is explicitly not being asserted.
  - Routing: name the seat when the question clearly belongs to a lens other
    than the PhD's (§26). The PhD is point; the others are not bypassed.

WHAT THIS RULE IS NOT
  - Not permission to skip the hard part and ask instead. Effort first.
  - Not a way to hand the proprietor a decision menu about something the
    executor is supposed to be expert in. Research it, then come back with a
    recommendation.
  - Not a reason to stall. Everything that does NOT depend on the answer gets
    finished while the question is open, and the parts that do depend on it are
    named as blocked rather than quietly half-built.

MECHANISM: the request goes in the DIRECTIVE block at the top of this file,
which is the council's activation trigger (§24). An executor log alone summons
nobody. The council answers, the executor decides, and the record shows which
input won and why.

Roster: 5 style advisors (haiku, sealed, PhD-facing format, per-question
routing per §26: Q1->Pragmatist, Q3->Expansionist) — closed by The PhD (opus).

HEADLINE FINDING, not asked for, more consequential than all three questions:
THE LEAKAGE SCANNER MISSES VERBATIM DUPLICATION 9 TIMES IN 10. leakage.py
samples 50-char shingles at a fixed byte stride starting at position 0 of
each text. A val document byte-identical to something in train is detected
only when the two copies happen to land on the same stride phase (mod 10).
MEASURED: one real document placed verbatim into train at all 10 possible
byte offsets -> detected at exactly 1 of 10 phases (100% at that phase, 0%
at the other nine) = 10% detection rate on a case that should read 100%
every time. This is not the C6 "wording" issue round 9 filed it under — it
is a detection failure in the scanner's core mechanism, one level below the
threshold question the DIRECTIVE actually asked. Line-overlap catches this
same case (99-100%), which is the only reason the tool has not looked
broken in testing so far — and the docstring names SHINGLE overlap as "the
one to trust." VERIFIED FIX: content-defined selection (keep a window iff
crc32(window) % 10 == 0) is phase-invariant (10/10 phases, 100% each,
measured). But the naive mod-p form has its own known flaw (Schleimer,
Wilkerson & Aiken 2003 — venue/pagination confirmed, the specific critique
is search-level not primary-text-verified this round): unbounded gaps
between selected fingerprints. Measured on this corpus: max gap 83 chars,
so a copied passage shorter than that can fall entirely between samples.
Winnowing (min-hash in a sliding window) fixes both defects and was
measured CHEAPER than the current sampler (20,580 fingerprints vs. 54,897,
bounded max gap 51 chars). This should be fixed before the null-replacement
work from round 9, not after — calibrating a broken detector with a good
statistical null produces a well-calibrated broken detector.

RANKED CRITIQUE (most decisive first)

1. (THE PHD — the headline finding above.) Ship the winnowing fix before
   anything else in this response. Everything below assumes the underlying
   shingle mechanism is trustworthy; right now it is not.

2. (THE PHD — Q2, EXECUTED on a real synthetic corpus, not reasoned about)
   THE SHIPPED 0.20/0.05 THRESHOLDS ARE ACTIVELY WRONG ON A DIFFERENTLY-
   SHAPED CORPUS, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. Built a real 10,000-document synthetic
   corpus with the project's own code and ran the actual scanner against it.
   A KNOWN-CLEAN split with ordinary shared boilerplate reads SUSPECT at
   19.1% — one hundredth of a point from CONTAMINATED, with ZERO actual
   contamination. A low-redundancy corpus with 20% of its validation
   documents duplicated verbatim into training reads CLEAN. The permutation
   null round 9 prescribed gets both cases right (p=0.119 on the false
   alarm, p=0.005 on the miss) — this is not a nicety, on a differently-
   shaped corpus the fixed constants are wrong and the error direction
   depends on inter-document redundancy, a property the tool never
   measures. CONFIRMS round 9's finding 1 should ship. FOUR corrections to
   round 9's specific recipe: (a) use the EXACT permutation p-value,
   (1+#{null>=obs})/(1+B) [Phipson & Smyth 2010, search-level verification],
   never a z-score — some corpora produce a null with sd=0.0000% exactly
   (a genuine zero-redundancy corpus, not a broken null — distinguished by
   measurement from character-shuffle's degenerate zero), and dividing by
   that sd is undefined; (b) B by wall-clock budget, not a fixed constant —
   measured cost scales with corpus SIZE not document count (~30ms/MB/perm:
   2.1s at 550KB, 31s at 5.3MB, ~10min projected at 100MB), and round 9's
   "~0.34s" holds only near the current corpus's size; (c) refuse to report
   below the feasibility cliff — derived, not guessed: the null collapses
   to one degenerate draw below ~1/val_frac documents (10, at the shipped
   val_frac=0.1), confirmed at n=3/5/8 docs (0.0000% null, health=False);
   (d) fix the sampler (finding 1) before trusting any of this.

3. (THE PHD — Q3, corrects round 9's own prior recipe AND the Expansionist's
   proposal this round) THE SEQUENTIAL-STOPPING IDEA IS SOUND MATH POINTED
   AT THE WRONG TARGET; A REBALANCED FIXED DESIGN BEATS BOTH THE ORIGINAL
   RECIPE AND THE ALTERNATIVE. Two audit results, both simulated: (a) the
   Expansionist's OTHER proposal this round (stop Q1's LR sweep once the
   top-2 "look separated") is the classic peeking trap — simulated false-
   positive inflation 3.5x-6.3x over nominal at N=5..25 under a true-null
   condition. His width-based stopping rule for Q3 avoids that specific
   trap, but (b) it introduces a worse one for this specific tool: it stops
   precisely when the sample happens to look quiet, which SYSTEMATICALLY
   UNDERSTATES the noise floor — simulated at his stated parameters,
   average stopping point N=9.55, noise-floor estimate biased -29.6% low,
   vs. -3.9% for a plain fixed N=8 design of comparable size. For a tool
   whose entire purpose is "tell the user how much of their delta is
   noise," a downward-biased noise floor is the one failure mode that must
   never happen — it makes real noise look like a real effect. REJECTED.
   Separately, round 9's own 5-splits-x-5-seeds=25-run recipe is also
   suboptimal, independent of the stopping-rule question: corrected
   variance-components simulation shows 10 splits x 2 seeds = 20 runs beats
   it on BOTH variance components (25.9% vs 36.0% relative error on the
   split component, 21.7% vs 29.3% total) at 20% less compute, and if only
   the total noise floor is wanted, 13 splits x 1 seed = 13 runs beats 25
   runs at roughly half the cost (20.4% vs 29.3% relative error). This
   independently reconfirms round 9's "vary the split before the seed"
   finding — a 2-splits-x-10-seeds design was the single worst configuration
   tested.

4. (THE PHD — Q1, with a real power calculation the Contrarian asked for
   and a real timing test of the Expansionist's sequential idea applied
   here) NO PRINCIPLED BASIS FOR SHRINKING THE GRID EXISTS IN THE
   LITERATURE (checked, not found); A PRINCIPLED BASIS FOR THE STEP COUNT
   DOES, AND IT SAYS SHRINKING IS SAFE BUT CHANGES THE CLAIM. Power
   calculation (200k-draw Monte Carlo against the prereg's actual
   conjunction bar): the canonical result's implicit false-positive rate is
   effectively zero over 400,000 simulated null draws, and the minimum
   detectable effect at 80% power (0.0213) sits almost exactly on the
   0.020 bar — the observed effect (0.0439) clears it 2.1x over. Noise
   would need to inflate 8x before power drops to 50%. Separately measured
   (68 real training runs across 4 step counts): the gap DECAYS
   monotonically and steeply with step count — 0.394 (400 steps) to 0.044
   (2000 steps), a 9x change — while the 7-point and 4-point sweep grids
   pick the identical winning LR at every step count tested. This CONFIRMS
   the Contrarian's "800 steps might be mid-convergence" and REFUTES his
   "the smaller grid might miss the optimum." Applied the Expansionist's
   sequential-stopping idea directly to this problem and it fails on
   arithmetic, not principle: stage 1 (the sweep) is not sequential-
   testable in the way that helps, because at 2000 steps stage 1 alone
   (7 runs, 2 worker-waves) already exceeds the 60s budget before a single
   confirmation run — sequential stopping on stage 2 saves at most ~6 runs
   (162s -> ~105s), nowhere near 60s. No published LR-sweep grid convention
   was found (checked muP/Yang et al. directly; it says nothing about grid
   spacing) that would license 7 points -> 4 on principle rather than
   convenience.

5. (STYLE ADVISORS + PhD, unanimous conclusion, now with numbers instead of
   just instinct) DO NOT SHRINK THE CANONICAL EXPERIMENT. All 5 style
   advisors independently converged on "ship canonical unchanged; any fast
   number must be a separate, clearly-labeled, never-substituting profile"
   — genuine unanimity, not vote-counting, and the PhD's numbers now show
   why: the estimand actually changes with step count (it's not the same
   claim, faster — it's a different, weaker claim), so a "faster canonical"
   is not achievable without becoming dishonest about what it measures. THE
   RECOMMENDATION: ship a second, HONESTLY-DIFFERENT preregistration at 800
   steps / 4 LRs / 5 seeds = 14 runs, ~56s (reachable, measured) — its claim
   is "treatment reaches lower train loss FASTER," not "the same effect,
   cheaper." Enforce the labeling mechanically (write step count and grid
   size into the result JSON's own verdict string) rather than trusting a
   human convention not to conflate the two six months from now — this
   directly answers the Contrarian's "labels get forgotten" objection.
   Add step count to the prereg's own NON-CLAIMS block; the measured 9x
   decay makes it a live limitation, not a hypothetical one. The Outsider's
   framing (a structural collision between an external deadline and a rule
   built to prevent exactly this) is correct as a description of the
   tension, and resolves cleanly once the fast profile is understood to
   answer a genuinely different, legitimate question rather than a cheaper
   version of the same one.

smallest in each sliding window of 25) rather than by position, so the same
passage yields the same fingerprints wherever it starts. Guarantees any shared
run of >= 74 characters is caught, which fixed-stride sampling never promised.

32. COUNCIL RESPONSE  (2026-07-26 ~22:50 — activation #11; scope: THIS FILE +
    localllm/leakage.py, localllm/data.py, localllm/exp_lr_width.py,
    localllm/prereg_lr_width_fast.json at HEAD, plus live probes/measurements
    run by The PhD on this machine, all logged below under EVIDENCE READ)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Roster: 5 style advisors (haiku, sealed, PhD-facing format, per-question
routing per §26: Q2->Expansionist, Q3->Contrarian) — closed by The PhD (opus).

HEADLINE FINDING, not asked for, and it is the second time this exact class
has appeared in two rounds: THE NEW WINNOWING FINGERPRINTER HAS ITS OWN
HASH-COLLISION BIAS, CAUSING FALSE CONTAMINATION ALARMS ON A LARGE, GENUINELY
CLEAN CORPUS. Winnowing keeps the MINIMUM hash per window; minima of crc32
(32-bit) are not uniform — measured on 1MB of random text, hash mean is
0.5002 of the full range but SELECTED fingerprint mean is 0.0556, a 9.0x
collision inflation. Measured false-overlap on two independently-generated,
genuinely-clean random corpora: 0.0078% at 500KB rising to 0.0706% at 4MB;
extrapolated from the measured effective hash range, a corpus with ZERO real
contamination crosses the still-shipped SUSPECT=0.05 threshold at roughly
500MB on hash collisions alone. Fix verified: swap crc32 for a 64-bit hash
(blake2b-64) — same fingerprint count (38,348 vs 38,326), false overlap
drops to 0.0000% at every measured size. One-line fix. This does not
invalidate the permutation-null design from rounds 9-10 (the collision floor
appears identically in the observed statistic and every permuted one, so the
p-value stays valid) — it invalidates the printed RAW FRACTION, which the
scanner's own docstring calls "the one to trust."

RANKED CRITIQUE (most decisive first)

1. (THE PHD — correction to last round's own headline finding, load-bearing)
   THE CONTRARIAN'S "CORRELATED SIGNAL MASKING" DIAGNOSIS OF THE PHASE BUG
   WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECT DIAGNOSIS IS WORSE. Read the pre-fix code
   directly (git show): the verdict property read shingle_frac ALONE — line
   overlap and doc overlap were printed but never consulted by the decision.
   There was no redundant code path masking the failure; the masking
   happened in the HUMAN reading the report next to a number that happened
   to look fine. This matters because it changes what a self-test must
   assert on: the DECISION (Report.verdict / exit code), not a sub-metric
   that happens to correlate with it — testing the sub-metric would have
   missed this bug even with a test in place.

2. (THE PHD — the round's single most consequential empirical result) THE
   OUTSIDER'S OWN PROPOSED TEST SUITE FOR "WHAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS"
   WOULD HAVE PASSED ON THE BUGGY CODE. Measured directly: his four offsets
   (0/100/500/1000) are all multiples of the sampler's STRIDE=10 and detect
   at 100% on the OLD, BROKEN sampler — his hand-picked "obvious" test is
   exactly the kind of test that misses this exact bug, because humans pick
   round numbers and round numbers are stride-aligned. The Pragmatist's
   partially-adversarial list (0/13/997/random) does better (3 of 4 catch
   it) precisely because it isn't all round numbers. This resolves the
   round's Q3 disagreement empirically rather than philosophically: the
   general principle is "when an implementation parameter (stride, block
   size, window) could align with a test-input parameter, the test input
   must be chosen adversarially or randomly, never by hand" — not "write
   more tests" in the abstract, and not "one hand-crafted test is enough"
   either, since the specific hand-crafted test proposed this round
   would not have worked.

3. (THE PHD — Q1, resolves a real split in the round with real statistics,
   not instinct) THE "REFUSE VS. CAVEAT" DISAGREEMENT HAS AN EXACT ANSWER:
   B >= 1/alpha - 1 IS A HARD FEASIBILITY BOUNDARY, DERIVED NOT CHOSEN. The
   permutation p-value is exactly valid at ANY B (Phipson & Smyth 2010,
   abstract-level primary-source verified this round: "valid for any number
   of permutations and samples") — a small B is underpowered, not wrong.
   But rejection at level alpha is mathematically impossible below
   B = 1/alpha - 1 (19 at alpha=0.05, 99 at alpha=0.01) REGARDLESS OF THE
   DATA. Below that floor: NO VERDICT, exit code 2, never CLEAN — printing
   CLEAN there would affirm a null the test structurally could not have
   rejected. At or above the floor: the p-value is fully valid and a
   verdict is licensed; refusing there is false modesty, resolving First
   Principles' and the Contrarian's softer "just caveat it" instinct as too
   weak in the infeasible case and too cautious in the feasible one.
   ADDITIONAL, uncovered by any advisor: a 2026 preprint (Cha, Lee, Schrab &
   Kim, arXiv:2605.03886 — HTML fetched, single preprint, not peer-reviewed,
   flagged accordingly) proves power is non-monotonic in B and that
   (B+1)*alpha being an integer is a local power maximum — meaning the
   executor's own example number, B=20, is a WORSE choice than B=19 at
   alpha=0.05 (cheaper AND more powerful). Also corrects the round's own
   premise: the "10 minutes at 100MB" budget concern was computed at B=200;
   the actual FEASIBILITY floor (B=19-99 for realistic alpha) costs 1-5
   minutes at 100MB, not 10 — the framing overstated the problem it was
   trying to solve.

4. (THE PHD — Q2, corrects the round toward a subtler bug than either
   proposed answer addressed) THE CHEAP "13x1" DESIGN COMMITS THE SAME SIN
   THE PHD RULED OUT LAST ROUND, VIA A DIFFERENT ROUTE, UNLESS SEED IS ALSO
   RANDOMIZED. Neither the Pragmatist's "ship 13x1 as default" nor the
   Contrarian's framing named whether the 13 splits share ONE fixed seed
   (biased low as a total-noise estimate, since init-seed variance is
   excluded by construction) or 13 independently-seeded splits (unbiased).
   Fix costs nothing — same 13 runs, same ~3.3 minutes, randomize both axes
   together (Bouthillier et al. 2021, primary-source verified: "adding more
   sources of variation... approaches the ideal estimator at 51x less
   compute"). The Expansionist's progressive-disclosure idea (report cheap
   now, upgrade in background) is a genuinely good idea SCOPED WRONG: the
   background-thread/queue architecture it needs is verified to already
   exist in studio.py (TrainWorker + _drain, used twice) and would be
   near-free there — but for a CLI tool whose whole discipline is
   preregistered numbers quoted as evidence, a result that silently changes
   after being copied into a log is the exact failure this project's own
   gates exist to prevent. Keep progressive disclosure as a future GUI
   feature, not a CLI default. Simplest fix that serves the Expansionist's
   real concern ("does hiding it behind a flag teach users it doesn't
   matter"): name the decomposition flag IN the default output's own text,
   not just in --help — a pointer a user reads beats an option they never
   knew existed, at the cost of one line.

5. (THE PHD — Q3, resolves the round's real disagreement in KIND, not just
   in content) BOTH THE "WRITE PROPERTY TESTS" AND "YOU'RE OVERTHINKING IT"
   CAMPS WERE HALF RIGHT. The Outsider is right that zero test
   infrastructure exists (verified: zero test files, no CI config, no
   pytest/hypothesis/mutmut in the venv) and that "the PhD read the code"
   is luck, not a system — but his own proposed fix doesn't survive contact
   with finding 2 above. The real answer sits under a real, findable
   literature the Contrarian's specific mechanism doesn't quite name but
   was reaching for: Voas 1992's PIE model (a fault must be Executed,
   Infect the state, and Propagate to output — the gap here was in
   propagation, into a human, not a redundant code path per finding 1), the
   general "oracle problem" (Barr et al. 2015 — there is no ground truth for
   an arbitrary corpus's true contamination), and METAMORPHIC TESTING
   (Chen et al. 1998, surveyed Segura et al. 2016) — built exactly for the
   no-oracle case, and "prepending N bytes must not change what the
   detector finds" is a textbook metamorphic relation. CORRECTION TO THE
   PROJECT'S OWN RECORD: an earlier claim that this project already flagged
   "mutation-adequacy on test suites" as queued work from "round 7" is
   WRONG — it is from council pass #3 (2026-07-25) and referred to a
   different artifact (the DPO track's injected unit tests, a track the
   project has since moved away from), not this toolkit's own source. The
   analogy still applies, and applies harder than claimed: the project
   understood "a test suite that lets a wrong answer pass is worthless" for
   its data and never applied the same standard to its own code, until now.

6. (THE PHD, additional finding, EXECUTED) THE 74-CHARACTER GUARANTEE IS
   TRUE BUT THE DOCSTRING OVERCLAIMS BY OMISSION. Measured: 74 chars -> 100%
   detected, 73 -> 99%, 60 -> 59%, 50 chars -> 4% — winnowing trades away
   short-run detection for phase-invariance, and at 50 characters the OLD,
   now-replaced sampler actually detected MORE (40%) than the new one does.
   The current docstring reads as unconditional dominance ("which
   fixed-stride sampling cannot promise at all"); it should carry the
   measured curve. General rule proposed and adopted: any docstring using
   the word "guarantee" or "the one to trust" must name the specific test
   that checks the claim, or not use the word.

    test statistic.
  - Phipson & Smyth's exact p-value FORMULA has now failed primary-source
    body verification twice (round 9 search-level, round 11 abstract-level
    only) — the ABSTRACT's validity claim is solid; the precise estimator
    form should be confirmed from the paper body or a secondary source
    (e.g. the statmod::permp documentation) before it's load-bearing for
    anything more specific than "any B is valid."

    — this round specified it in detail (no framework, ~40 lines, keep the

    the stride/round-number alignment risk finding 2 identified as the
    general mechanism (e.g. batch_size, block_size, eval_interval) — this
    round found the specific instance, not a sweep for the class.

THE QUESTIONS THEMSELVES: this is now the THIRD round in a row where the
DIRECTIVE's own questions were good but a mechanism-level check turned up
something bigger than any of them (round 9: the seed no-op; round 10: the
phase-blind sampler; round 11: the new fingerprinter's own collision bias).
That is not bad luck three times — it is what happens when questions are
asked about TUNING a tool (thresholds, run counts, test coverage) before
the tool's newest layer has been independently re-verified. Worth stating
as a standing checklist item rather than re-discovering it a fourth time:
any DIRECTIVE about a just-modified measurement mechanism should default to
"verify the new mechanism first" even when — especially when — the round's
actual ask is about something one level up from the mechanism itself.

YOUR HEADLINE WAS A BUG IN CODE I SHIPPED THIS SESSION, hours old. Reproduced
before fixing. Winnowing keeps the MINIMUM hash per window, so the effective
hash space is the bottom of the range rather than the whole of it, and 32-bit
crc32 does not have enough room down there.
  RED   selected fingerprints averaged 0.0557 of the range against 0.5018 for
        all hashes (9.0x concentration, matching your measurement). Two
        INDEPENDENTLY generated, genuinely clean corpora with zero real overlap:
        0.0065% false at 200KB, 0.0081% at 800KB, 0.0293% at 2MB. Rising with
        size, toward the still-shipped 5% SUSPECT threshold.
  GREEN blake2b-64: 0.0000% at every size measured, same fingerprint counts,
        phase invariance still 10/10.
One line, exactly as you specified.

FIRST TESTS THIS REPOSITORY HAS EVER HAD: +localllm/test_detectors.py, no
framework, `python test_detectors.py`. Built to your finding 1 and finding 2
rather than to my instinct:
  - ASSERTS ON THE DECISION (Report.verdict), not a sub-metric. Your correction
    that the masking happened in the HUMAN reading a healthy neighbouring number,

34. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-26 — runlog.py; short entry, state only)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
One thing shipped since §33, so the record is not behind:

+localllm/runlog.py — append-only run log, runs.jsonl. Every training run,
experiment, benchmark and leakage scan now appends one record: timestamp, device,
corpus FINGERPRINT (sha1 + size, so runs on different text are never compared),
model config, final train/val loss, wall clock, ms/step, and the leakage verdict
next to any val loss. Nothing is overwritten. `python runlog.py` prints a
summary; `--review` prints a digest for someone who did not watch the runs.

Wired into train.py, studio.py (GUI runs included), leakage.py, exp_lr_width.py
and bench_device.py. Logging is wrapped so a failure to record can never take
down a run that otherwise succeeded.

WHY: result files that get overwritten by the next run cannot show a trend, and
the numbers quoted in these logs came from terminals that have since closed. The
proprietor intends to accumulate training runs between sessions, so the evidence
has to survive without either of us remembering to save it.

STATE: no change to §33's open list. The permutation null, the noise-floor
utility, and the split_health wiring are all still unbuilt.

35. COUNCIL RESPONSE  (2026-07-26 ~19:35 — activation #12; scope: THIS FILE +
    localllm/leakage.py, localllm/test_detectors.py, localllm/bench_device.py,
    localllm/data.py, localllm/train.py, localllm/exp_lr_width.py at HEAD,
    plus live probes/measurements run by The PhD on this machine)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTE ON THIS ROUND: the PhD's first attempt was cut off mid-analysis by an API
session limit and produced no usable findings; it was re-run from scratch in
full. Separately, a git branch mix-up briefly checked this working directory
out to the public `main` branch, making this file disappear from disk for
part of the round — resolved by the proprietor/executor before this response
was written; the DIRECTIVE and history below were re-confirmed unchanged
against the restored `protocol/merge-moonwalker` branch before use.

Roster: 5 style advisors (haiku, sealed, PhD-facing format, per-question
routing per §26: Q3->Contrarian) — closed by The PhD (opus).

HEADLINE FINDING, not asked for: THE SHIPPED TRAINER (train.py) HAS A
REPRODUCIBILITY BUG THE PROJECT'S OWN EXPERIMENT HARNESS ALREADY SOLVED AND
NEVER CARRIED OVER. train.py's estimate_loss() draws eval batches from the
SAME global torch RNG as training (no dedicated Generator) — so how often you
evaluate changes what you train on. Measured directly: at eval_interval
248/249/250/251/252/500, final loss varies by 0.004361, comparable to the
measured seed-to-seed noise floor (0.005359, ~0.81x) at this scale — a real
defect, not yet a wrong-conclusion one. Isolating eval onto its own Generator
(the fix) produces BIT-IDENTICAL loss (spread 0.000000) regardless of
eval_interval. The fix already exists in this repo: exp_lr_width.py's
fixed_eval_batches(), whose own docstring says "drawn from our own Generator
rather than the global RNG, so evaluating can never disturb the training
stream" — the experiment harness got this right and the shipped trainer
never inherited it. Two-line fix, red witness above.

RANKED CRITIQUE (most decisive first)

1. (THE PHD — Q1, the round's most consequential empirical result) THE
   DETECTOR SCORES 0% RECALL ON A REAL PYTHON CLONE TYPE, AND THE CAUSE IS
   MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Built a clone-type recall table (Type-1/2/3 per
   the code-clone-detection literature, mechanically via Python's own
   tokenize — not hand-tuned parameters) on the project's own 10 real
   corpus documents: verbatim 100%, Type-1 (reformat) 90%, Type-2 (full
   identifier rename) 0%, Type-3 (Type-2 + 10% statement edits) 0%.
   Factorial isolation pinpointed the cause: renaming EVERY identifier in
   a file costs almost nothing (22.4% -> 24.1% overlap); replacing string
   literals (docstrings/strings, ~7.4% of this corpus) drops overlap to
   0.0%. On this codebase, ~7% of the text (prose inside the code) carries
   essentially all surviving detection signal; the 92% that is actual
   logic contributes almost none once whitespace is re-rendered. This
   directly answers and sharpens the executor's own named gap ("always
   says CLEAN on real near-duplicates") -- it is not a marginal miss, it
   is total, on an entire clone class.
   RESOLVES the round's real disagreement: First Principles was right that
   self-authored mutation tests relocate the same blind spot ("15% token
   replacement" is a number a person likes, exactly the anti-pattern that
   caused the last two bugs) -- but the fix is not abandoning mutation
   testing, it's sourcing the mutation DEFINITIONS from outside (the
   published Type-1/2/3 taxonomy) rather than inventing them. The specific
   external labeled benchmarks proposed (BigCloneBench: Java-only, doesn't
   run here; a documented 2022 critique found 86% false positives in its
   contested clone types anyway; SemanticCloneBench/GPTCloneBench: the
   wrong instrument, it's a Type-4 *semantic* benchmark and this detector
   would correctly score ~0% on it, which would misreport as failure) are
   both dead ends for different reasons -- the genuinely applicable
   literature is LLM-training-data dedup, not clone detection: Lee et al.
   2021/ACL 2022 (verified at the byte, fetched the paper text directly)
   gives a published external number for exactly this blind spot on
   natural text -- "77% of the training examples NEARDUP removes from C4
   have a verbatim length-50 match EXACTSUBSTR also finds" -- meaning a
   verbatim-only detector misses ~23% of near-duplicates on PROSE; this
   project's own measurement shows it is far worse on CODE (0%) because
   code survives identifier renaming much more completely than prose
   survives paraphrase. That same paper's own methodology answers "what do
   you do without an oracle": triangulate with a SECOND, different-
   failure-mode detector and report disagreement, not chase a labeled set.

2. (THE PHD — Q1, the tool's core claim must change) THE SHIPPED REPORT
   LINE ("Validation looks independent of training. Val loss is usable.")
   IS CONTRADICTED BY FINDING 1 AND MUST BE DELETED, NOT SOFTENED. A CLEAN
   verdict means no VERBATIM leakage of >=74 characters was found -- it
   says nothing about renamed or rewritten copies, which finding 1 shows
   pass through entirely undetected. Recommended replacement claim,
   scoped honestly: "finds verbatim shared runs >=74 chars at any offset,
   100% recall (measured); does NOT find renamed or rewritten copies,
   measured 0% recall on identifier-renamed Python; this is an exact-
   substring scanner, not a near-duplicate scanner." Also corrects the
   test suite's own docstring, which claims "every test runs against both
   implementations, the old one must FAIL" -- verified: 3 of 4 shipped
   tests PASS on the known-broken old sampler; only the phase-invariance
   test enforces the both-implementations rule. The suite's actual
   guarantee (4/4 pass, exit 0) is true at the byte; its documented
   guarantee is not.

3. (THE PHD — Q2, EXECUTED sensitivity sweep, not just evaluated) FOUR OF
   SIX NAMED PARAMETERS CLEARED ON MEASUREMENT (WINDOW, SHINGLE, block_size,
   batch_size -- no discontinuity found, ~4 minutes of probing), BUT THE
   MECHANICAL SWEEP METHOD ITSELF HAS A BLIND SPOT THAT COST TWO REAL
   DEFECTS. Corrected the Pragmatist's proposed method first: "hash the
   output, flag if it changes" fires on every parameter by construction
   (changing batch_size is SUPPOSED to change output) and discriminates
   nothing -- the right test is a discontinuity against the parameter's
   own neighborhood, which is what was actually run. Two real defects
   found this way, NEITHER of which a +/-1 sweep would have caught: (a)
   val_frac SATURATES at 0.14 regardless of what's requested (0.15 and
   0.20 both silently produce 13.73%, because a large-document corpus
   exhausts fillable budget under the current packing) -- and
   split_health()['ok'] returns True the ENTIRE time, because its own
   tolerance constant (achieved >= requested * 0.5) is itself another
   hand-picked number, and the guard built to catch exactly this passes
   silently; a wide sweep found this, a +/-1 sweep would not have. (b)
   eval_interval couples eval frequency to what gets trained via shared-
   RNG consumption (this response's headline finding) -- not a
   discontinuity at all but a hidden coupling, a class the neighborhood-
   sweep method cannot detect in principle. CONCLUSION on Q2's actual
   question: NEITHER "systematic sweep" nor "per-parameter reasoning"
   alone is sufficient -- the mechanical sweep clears a class (alignment/
   cliff risk) cheaply and should be built as a standing tool, but it
   structurally cannot find coupling-type or wide-saturation-type defects,
   which is exactly what First Principles' "document why every constant
   exists" would have surfaced (writing "eval_interval controls only how
   often we observe" forces the question of whether that's true -- it
   isn't).

4. (THE PHD — Q3, resolves a three-way split against real external
   practice) NO COMPARABLE LOCAL-LLM TOOL SILENTLY SUBSTITUTES A WEAKER
   MODEL FOR SLOW HARDWARE. Checked Ollama, llama.cpp, and LM Studio's
   actual behavior (search-level verification, not byte-fetched --
   Ollama's own FAQ returned 404 on direct fetch, flagged accordingly, but
   the recommendation doesn't hinge on precision here): all three ADAPT
   THE EXECUTION PLAN (GPU/CPU layer offload) rather than swap the asset,
   and LM Studio explicitly DISCLOSES a fit estimate before download
   rather than deciding for the user. This directly refutes the
   Pragmatist's specific auto-downsize threshold on its own terms: his
   proposed rule (CPU + estimated_time > 300s -> auto-downsize) fires on
   THIS PROJECT'S OWN measured "default" CPU time (324.2s, from
   bench_device_result.json) -- meaning the rule as written silently
   downgrades the DEFAULT SIZE ON THIS ACTUAL MACHINE, not just weak
   laptops, exactly the hidden-quality-tier the Outsider warned about,
   and the threshold itself is another hand-picked round number, the
   precise anti-pattern this whole round is about. Separately corrects
   the round's framing: bench_device.py's ALREADY-SHIPPED measurement is
   functionally LM Studio's pre-download fit estimate -- nothing new needs
   building, it needs to be SURFACED as a pre-selected choice with real
   numbers ("small 1.0 min / default 5.4 min / large 56.9 min, recommended:
   default") rather than either silently deciding or leaving the user with
   no estimate at all. Also corrects the round's generalization: "5.4
   minutes" was measured on a Ryzen 9 7900X (a fast desktop CPU); a CPU-
   ONLY user (no discrete GPU) is more likely on materially slower
   hardware, and the council was reasoning about the CPU population from
   the fastest CPU in the building -- the estimate-and-ask design in
   finding 4 sidesteps this by measuring the ACTUAL machine at install
   time rather than trusting one number for all CPUs.

5. (THE PHD, corrections that changed severity of two style-advisor
   claims) The Contrarian's specific worry about WINDOW=25 being another
   hand-picked, at-risk constant was checked directly (swept 21-29) and
   found NOT to hold -- the w+k-1 guarantee is exactly 100% at every
   window tested, so this particular suspicion is retired by measurement,
   though the broader instinct (distrust unexamined constants) is what
   correctly caught findings 3's two real defects elsewhere. Separately,
   the Contrarian's "0.20/0.05 thresholds are uncalibrated" is CONFIRMED
   and shown worse than stated: measured real Type-1/Type-2 clone overlap
   lands at 22.4%-26.3% -- 2 to 6 points ABOVE the CONTAMINATED line,
   meaning a real near-duplicate, on the rare occasion the detector
   catches any signal from it at all, gets its verdict decided by a
   threshold sitting inside the exact range where actual clones score.

EVIDENCE READ / EXECUTED THIS PASS:
  - localllm/leakage.py, test_detectors.py, bench_device.py, data.py,
    train.py, exp_lr_width.py (current HEAD) — orchestrator, confirmed
    branch protocol/merge-moonwalker and file contents before framing.
  - Clone-type recall table (verbatim/Type-1/Type-2/Type-3) on the real
    corpus, factorial isolation (rename-ids x replace-strings) — PhD,
    EXECUTED, using Python's own tokenize, no framework.
  - w+k-1 guarantee swept across WINDOW 21-29, SHINGLE 46-54 — PhD,
    EXECUTED, 100% at every value tested.
  - block_size and batch_size neighborhood sweeps (GPU + CPU ms/step) —
    PhD, EXECUTED, no discontinuity found at either.
  - EVAL_BATCHES CI-vs-effect-size calculation (10-160) — PhD, EXECUTED.
  - val_frac sweep 0.06-0.20 — PhD, EXECUTED, found the saturation cliff
    at 0.14 and the split_health tolerance-constant bug.
  - eval_interval RNG-coupling isolation (248/249/250/251/252/500, vs.
    seed-noise floor, vs. isolated-Generator control) — PhD, EXECUTED,
    this response's headline finding.
  - test_detectors.py's actual pass/fail behavior against the old broken
    sampler, all 4 tests individually — PhD, EXECUTED, found 3/4 pass on
    known-broken code, contradicting the suite's own docstring.
  - shingles() memory/timing scaling — PhD, EXECUTED, linear to tested
    sizes, extrapolated cost noted at 1GB.
  - Time-estimate stability across a 16x corpus-size range, CPU and GPU —
    PhD, EXECUTED (answers the Expansionist's [VERIFY]).
  - Lee et al. 2021/ACL 2022 — PhD, fetched and quoted directly from the
    paper text, not abstract-only.
  - Schleimer/Wilkerson/Aiken 2003 — PhD, formula confirmed via search,
    independently reconfirmed by this round's own w+k-1 sweep.
  - Krinke & Ragkhitwetsagul 2022, Svajlenko & Roy 2015/2016, GPTCloneBench
    (arXiv:2308.13963), Ollama/llama.cpp/LM Studio auto-sizing behavior —

    as byte-verified.
  READ-ONLY: nothing else this pass beyond what's listed above.

LEFTOVER RISKS (unresolved even with every fix above landed):
  - The eval-RNG fix (headline finding) is specified with an exact patch
    target (train.py's estimate_loss, copy exp_lr_width.py's
    fixed_eval_batches) but not yet applied — train.py still couples eval
    frequency to training data as of this round.
  - The clone-type recall numbers rest on only 10 usable corpus documents
    — the DIRECTION is unambiguous (0% vs 100% overlap is not a close
    call) but the precise recall percentages should be re-measured on a
    larger corpus before being quoted as calibrated figures rather than a
    documented capability gap.
  - The proposed second, NEARDUP-class detector (MinHash, Lee et al.'s
    published constants) is recommended but not built — until it exists,
    this project has exactly one detector with exactly one failure mode
    and no disagreement signal to check it against.
  - val_frac's saturation fix and split_health's tolerance-constant fix
    are specified but not built; a user requesting 20% validation still
    silently receives 13.73% with a passing health check as of this round.
  - The mechanical discontinuity-sweep tool (finding 3's recommendation)
    does not exist as a standing repo tool yet — this round ran it once,
    by hand, in scratch scripts.
  - Ollama/llama.cpp/LM Studio's exact auto-sizing behavior was search-
    verified, not confirmed at each project's primary documentation byte
    — if this becomes load-bearing for the actual installer implementation
    rather than a design-direction argument, re-verify at the primary
    source first.
  - No one has checked whether the eval-RNG coupling (headline finding)
    also affects exp_lr_width.py's OWN production code path beyond the
    isolated control probe — the fix was verified to work in principle,
    not yet verified end-to-end in the shipped experiment script.

THE QUESTIONS THEMSELVES: the executor explicitly applied the standing
"verify the mechanism before tuning it" checklist item to himself this
round before the council was even asked — the DIRECTIVE said so directly.
That is the checklist working as intended, and it is why this round's three
questions were already well-aimed. The gap this round exposes is different
and worth naming for future DIRECTIVEs: a "did I test this enough" question
about a detector or measurement tool should explicitly ask "where did the
TEST'S OWN PARAMETERS come from" (hand-picked, like the mutation
percentages that were nearly adopted here) as a standing sub-question,
not just whether tests exist or pass. A test suite authored entirely by
the same person who ships the bugs inherits that person's blind spots
one level up, and this round found that pattern trying to re-enter through
mutation testing minutes after it had just been evicted from sampling.

   false-positive test with disjoint halves of the real corpus. (3) Fix

   None` by design, "If None, no weighting is applied and the loss is the same

   READ-ONLY"): name the file+commit this audit refers to, or confirm it never

   isn't there.

2. (READ-ONLY) TASK 1/2'S REAL PROBLEM IS PROVENANCE DRIFT, NOT "IS IT
   VERIFIED" — AND IT'S A LEGITIMATE GAP. verify_dataset.py never reads
   dpo_pairs_capped.jsonl (grep confirms — only dpo_pairs.jsonl and
   repair_pairs.jsonl appear in FILES). build_training_set.py:42-63 samples
   the capped file straight from dpo_pairs.jsonl's rows unmodified — no
