64. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - R4 and R1 executed. The held-out bank is SPENT,
    measured. The training seam is closed with a red witness. R3 is no longer a
    judgement call.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
R4 - THE HELD-OUT BANK IS SPENT. Measured, not inferred.

Base model (llama3-forged-null, which section 61 confirmed is configurationally
identical to the stock base) on all 10 held-out tasks, 20 samples each, same
actor path / ACTOR_SYSTEM / verifier / temperature policy as eval.py:

     k    pass@k        gcd            20/20     rotate     0/20
     1    0.8700        is_prime       20/20     rle       18/20
     3    0.8999        reverse_words  20/20     nth_fib   17/20
     5    0.9000        flatten        20/20     kadane    19/20
    10    0.9000        count_vowels   20/20
    20    0.9000        dedup          20/20

pass@20 == pass@5 == pass@3 == 0.9000 EXACTLY. Nine of ten tasks are solvable;
rotate is solvable at no sampling budget up to 20; six tasks are at 20/20.

THE CONSEQUENCE, stated as arithmetic. The ceiling on this bank is 0.90. The base
already sits at 0.87 pass@1. So THE ENTIRE MEASURABLE RANGE IS 3 PERCENTAGE
POINTS, against section 62's corrected MDE of ~2.6pp. One detectable step in the
whole instrument. Section 61's held-out null (-0.8pp) was never evidence about
training: this ruler could not have registered more than +3pp if run 1 had been
perfect.

This closes section 62 leftover-risk 5 and gives Yue et al. (arXiv:2504.13837) a
concrete local number. Their claim is that verifier-grounded training re-ranks
what the base already contains. Here the base contains solutions to 9/10 tasks
within 20 samples, so training can only convert latent capability into pass@1,
and there are three points of it to convert. Whether that generalises is their
question, not mine; what is measured here is that MY bank is spent.

R3 IS THEREFORE NOT A JUDGEMENT CALL, IT IS A PRECONDITION. Section 63 reversed
section 58's screen shutdown on section 62's variance argument. R4 replaces the
argument with a measurement. The ruler must be replaced before any run whose
conclusion depends on a held-out number. rotate should not survive the
replacement: 0/20 is not a hard task, it is a dead channel.

R1 - THE TRAINING SEAM IS CLOSED. RED WITNESS, then GREEN, both kept.

  RED, the same assertions run against the pre-fix implementations:
    FAIL test_training_prompt_carries_the_system_turn (roles=['user'])
    FAIL test_training_system_message_is_the_sampling_system_message
         (no system turn at all)
    FAIL test_target_is_fenced_not_bare_source
         (target='def foo(x):\n    return'...)
    3 failed against the old code

  GREEN tests/test_training_seam.py -> 5 passed, 0 failed
        tests/test_screen_sizing.py -> 0 failed, no regression

I wrote the test AFTER the fix, so it started green and proved nothing. I then
ran its assertions against the old implementations to confirm they actually
bite. Stating that because a test written after a fix is decoration until it has
been shown to fail.

WHAT CHANGED, and why the fence half is additive rather than a replacement:
  - train_native.build_training_prompt() now renders system=ACTOR_SYSTEM + user,
    the same two turns forge.Actor.generate posts when sampling and eval.py
    posts when scoring.
  - forge.Result gains `raw`, defaulting to "" so every existing
    Result(code=...) call site is unaffected. run_task threads the verbatim
    completion into it.
  - forge writes chosen_raw / rejected_raw ALONGSIDE chosen / rejected.
    chosen/rejected MUST stay executable source because clean_dataset.py:26,
    verify_dataset.py:82 and both dedup hashes run forge.verify on them.
    Replacing them with fenced text would have broken all three gates. Additive
    on purpose.
  - train_native.training_target() prefers the verbatim completion and falls
    back to re-fencing the stored source for the 918 legacy pairs. THE FALLBACK
    IS A RECONSTRUCTION, NOT THE ORIGINAL BYTES - close, because ACTOR_SYSTEM
    forbids prose, but not identical. Pairs generated after this change carry
    the real thing.

  SCOPE: the test pins the two mismatches actually found. A third divergence
  introduced later is NOT caught automatically. Enumerated, not swept. The test
  uses a recording stand-in for the tokenizer, so it runs on plain python with
  no transformers, no download, no GPU.

WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO. It does not re-run anything. Every number banked in
sections 60, 61 and 63 was produced under the broken seam and none of them are
retro-corrected by this commit - they remain the record of what that
configuration did. The next run measures the fixed configuration, and R2 holds
prompt count at 13 precisely so that the seam is the only thing that moved.

REVISED ORDER OF WORK, changed by R4:
  1. Replace the held-out ruler. Fresh draw from the 25,862-row oss pool,
     selected for unsaturated tasks, NOT the admitted 30 (section 62
     leftover-risk 6: reusing the admitted set re-imports selection-on-noise
     into the ruler itself). This is now first because every downstream
     conclusion depends on a held-out number and the current one cannot produce
     one.
  2. Regenerate pairs at 13 prompts from the bnb-4bit artifact, with the seam
     closed and chosen_raw populated.
  3. Re-run at 13 prompts. Compare against sections 60/61 to isolate the seam.
  4. Only then rule on section 62's 200-prompt recommendation.

STILL PENDING, unchanged: R5 (rpo_alpha=0 ablation - the objective is DPO+NLL
and nothing separates the terms); the generator/trainer identity gap, whose
headline number section 62 correctly deflated 3x and whose bnb-4bit side is
still n=1; section 58 item 2's SFT/KTO/DPO question; the one-shot RLVR result.

NOT CHECKED THIS ROUND: I did not re-verify section 62's citations. I did not
measure whether the seam fix changes anything - that is step 3 and it has not
run. I did not check the TRL 0.12.2 double-BOS question section 62 raised, which
would compound the seam and is still open.

65. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - screen run 2 launch FAILED on the wrong
    interpreter. Root cause found, class closed, relaunched. Logged as its own
    section per the proprietor's one-run-one-section ruling, including because
    it failed.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FIRST LAUNCH OF THE RULER-REPLACEMENT SCREEN FAILED IMMEDIATELY.

  python screen_tasks.py --stratum oss --candidates 500 --target 78
  Traceback (most recent call last):
    File "screen_tasks.py", line 271, in <module>  main()
    File "screen_tasks.py", line 217, in main
      cands = [c for c in candidates(args.candidates, args.seed, args.stratum)
    File "screen_tasks.py", line 116, in candidates
      import pyarrow.parquet as pq
  ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pyarrow'

  Zero rows written; data/screen_results.jsonl unchanged at 24.

ROOT CAUSE, measured rather than guessed:
  default `python`              3.14.6   pyarrow ABSENT
  .venv-train/Scripts/python    3.11.9   pyarrow 25.0.0, requests present

screen_tasks.py reads AceCode's parquet shards, so it needs pyarrow, which lives
only in .venv-train. The repo's default interpreter is 3.14 because that is the
system Python; the training stack cannot use it, which is why .venv-train exists
at 3.11 in the first place. Nothing in screen_tasks.py said so, and the failure
surfaced a hundred lines in, from inside candidates(), AFTER argparse had
accepted every flag and printed nothing.

THE CLASS, and it was already solved elsewhere in this repo. export_adapter.py
carries _reexec_in_venv_if_needed() for exactly this reason - its own docstring
says "rather than fail on an import three steps in, hand off to the right
interpreter immediately. Same lesson as sync_public.py's test gate." So this is
the THIRD script to need the guard and the first two already had it. I did not
invent a new mechanism; I copied the one that was there (build from what's
there), keyed on pyarrow instead of torch/gguf/safetensors.

  RED   python screen_tasks.py --stratum oss --candidates 500 --target 78
        -> ModuleNotFoundError from inside candidates(), 0 rows written
  GREEN python screen_tasks.py --help
        -> re-execs into .venv-train and prints usage, exit 0
        tests/test_screen_sizing.py -> 0 failed, no regression

  SCOPE: the guard keys on pyarrow specifically. A future import that exists in
  neither interpreter still fails the old way, and a fourth script needing the
  same treatment is not fixed automatically. Enumerated, not swept. What would
  actually close the class is one shared entry-point helper the scripts import
  rather than three copies of the same function - noted as an open item, not
  done, because two copies existed before I arrived and a third does not justify
  a refactor of all three mid-run.

RELAUNCHED under .venv-train, same arguments, resuming against the 24 rows
already on disk (the screen skips already-screened ids, so the earlier partial
oss draw is reused rather than redrawn). Running now; its result gets its own
section.

WHY THIS IS A SECTION AT ALL. Proprietor ruling 2026-07-29: one run, one
numbered section, after every run rather than at milestones - and section 64 was
named as the violation that prompted it, having folded R4 and R1 together. A
failed launch is a run: it produced evidence (the interpreter split), it changed
the repo (the guard), and a future session that hits ModuleNotFoundError here
should find this rather than rediscover it.

NOT CHECKED: whether the other scripts in this repo that touch parquet, torch or
gguf all carry the guard. I checked export_adapter.py (has it) and screen_tasks.py
(now has it) and took sync_public.py's on the word of export_adapter.py's
docstring without opening it.

66. EXECUTOR LOG  (2026-07-29 - section 62's double-BOS non-claim CLOSED, and
    the answer is NEGATIVE: TRL 0.12.2 does not double-add for this model. The
    seam fix in section 63 is not compounded.)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 62 left this open: "I did not verify whether TRL 0.12.2's DPOTrainer
double-adds a BOS token on top of apply_chat_template's output, which is a known
class of bug in that vintage. That would require running the tokenizer.
Unchecked, and it would compound finding 2."

It would compound it because apply_chat_template already emits
<|begin_of_text|>. A second BOS would give every training sequence a prefix no
sampled sequence ever carried - the same defect class section 63 just closed,
one layer down. Ran the tokenizer. CPU only, no GPU, no weights, no writes.

THE HAZARD IS REAL AT THE TOKENIZER LEVEL:

  bos_token='<|begin_of_text|>' id=128000
  build_training_prompt() output starts with bos text : True
  tok(prompt)                            -> first ids [128000, 128000, 128006]  BOS count 2
  tok(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)  -> first ids [128000, 128006,   9125]  BOS count 1

So if anything tokenizes our prompt with library defaults, it DOES double-add.
The question was only ever whether TRL does.

IT DOES NOT, for this model. Read from the installed source, trl 0.12.2:

  dpo_trainer.py:580-582   prompt/chosen/rejected are each tokenized with
                           add_special_tokens=False
  dpo_trainer.py:585       if add_special_tokens:
                               prompt_input_ids = [tokenizer.bos_token_id] + prompt_input_ids
  dpo_trainer.py:473       "add_special_tokens": self.is_encoder_decoder

and for our base:

  unsloth/llama-3-8b-Instruct-bnb-4bit
    architectures      ['LlamaForCausalLM']
    is_encoder_decoder  False

So TRL binds add_special_tokens=False, the manual BOS-prepend branch never runs,
and the explicit add_special_tokens=False on all three fields prevents the
tokenizer from adding one either. Exactly one BOS reaches the model, supplied by
apply_chat_template, which is what the sampler also sent.

VERDICT: NEGATIVE FINDING. No defect. Section 63's seam fix stands unqualified -
the training sequence now matches the sampled sequence at the BOS/system-turn
level, and nothing downstream re-breaks it.

WHY A NEGATIVE FINDING GETS ITS OWN SECTION. Because it was a live open item that
would have invalidated the next run's interpretation, and "we checked and it was
fine" is only worth anything if the check is on the record with its evidence. An
unchecked hazard and a checked-clean hazard look identical in a summary and are
not the same thing. Also the one-run-one-section ruling does not have a
"good news" exemption.

SCOPE, and it is narrow. This is true for THIS model (decoder-only) and THIS TRL
version (0.12.2). A future encoder-decoder base would set is_encoder_decoder=True
and the branch WOULD fire on top of our template's BOS - so the safe reading is
not "TRL never double-adds", it is "TRL double-adds exactly when
is_encoder_decoder is True, and ours is False". A TRL upgrade re-opens this; the
binding at line 473 is the thing to re-read, not the conclusion.

NOT CHECKED: whether the EOS handling has an analogous issue (TRL appends
eos_token to completions; our chosen/rejected now end with a ``` fence rather
than <|eot_id|>, and whether that matters is unmeasured). Whether
max_prompt_length truncation interacts with the now-longer system-turn prompt -
section 62's A2 measured the OLD prompt at ~112 tokens against a 512 budget, and
the system turn adds ~47, so the headroom is still large but the measurement is
now stale by construction.

Screen run 2 continues in parallel: 33 screened, 8 admitted at the time of
writing. Its result gets its own section.

standing by
